> 1494	
> 1495	## Regras rápidas (injetadas nos agentes)
> 1496	
> 1497	1. Roteie o motor ANTES de escrever prompt: nó, número, UI, texto, grafo e esteira são motion (0 mco); rosto e matéria física são Veo; still é Nano — pedir texto ao Veo é dinheiro queimado.
> 1498	2. Não despache nenhum mco sem beat map com scene_no + roll (a_roll/b_roll) e busca de reuso na Biblioteca feita antes.
> 1499	3. Todo prompt de shot declara, nesta ordem: sujeito, ação/fala literal, lente (corpo + focal + T-stop), UM movimento de câmera, luz por aparato (key/fill/rim + ratio + temperatura + fonte motivada), atmosfera, paleta, película, negativos — nomeie o aparato, nunca o clima.
> 1500	4. Um movimento de câmera por take de 8s; dois viram purê.
> 1501	5. Nunca escreva parâmetros fisicamente contraditórios (14mm + shallow DOF, handheld + locked-off) — o motor escolhe sozinho e você paga para descobrir.
> 1502	6. Fala de take de 8s = 18 a 22 palavras em pt-BR (≈2,4 palavras/s); se não couber, corte a frase, não aumente o prompt.
> 1503	7. Respeite o contrato do Veo ou receba 422: refs sem first-frame exigem 8s, 1080p só com 8s, máximo 3 refs, text_to_video só 4/6/8s.
> 1504	8. Negativos = base fixa + 3 a 5 termos do plano, nunca mais de ~30 no total; texto na tela, legenda, logo, timecode e letterbox ficam sempre na base.
> 1505	9. Nano Banana devolve sempre 1:1 — resolva enquadramento em FFmpeg (crop com headroom, Ken Burns ou pillar-blur), nunca re-gerando.
> 1506	10. Antes de pedir retake, tente cor; depois tente composição; retake é o terceiro recurso e exige mudar o prompt, não repetir o mesmo pedido.
> 1507	11. Alpha nunca sobrevive a -pix_fmt yuv420p: faça overlay direto da sequência PNG RGBA, sem MP4 intermediário; se precisar de arquivo, use qtrle ou prores yuva444p10le.
> 1508	12. Ordem de imagem é imutável — correction, depois LUT do canal, depois grão; e grão/vinheta só uma vez (CSS ou FFmpeg, nunca os dois); todo master sai BT.709 limited com tagging explícito.
> 1509	13. Toda peça tem leito de room tone contínuo do primeiro ao último frame (−45..−38 LUFS); silêncio digital entre falas é defeito, não pausa.
> 1510	14. No bus de áudio: amix sempre com normalize=0, adelay sempre com valor por canal (X|X), aresample=48000 antes de atrasar, apad=whole_dur no final — e nunca corte duração por segmento.
> 1511	15. Diálogo é a âncora e nunca é ducado: SFX −6..−12 dB, trilha −15..−20 dB sob fala com sidechain alimentado pela fala SECA, ambiência −18..−25 dB.
> 1512	16. Master de episódio sai com loudnorm em DOIS passes (medir → aplicar linear=true) em I=−16 LUFS, TP=−1,5 dBTP, LRA=11 — passe único não acerta o alvo.
> 1513	17. Offsets de timeline vêm de ffprobe dos arquivos reais, nunca da duração planejada; quando congelam, é picture lock — mix, legenda e shorts só depois, e quebrar o lock invalida tudo em cascata.
> 1514	18. Nada é 'pronto' sem Visão E Audição na mesma timeline (contact sheet no Vision + whisper timestampado + ebur128); e onde a capacidade não existe (lip-sync com voz clonada, roto neural, 3D, outpainting, separação de stems), declare o GAP em vez de prometer.
> 1515	
> ```

> 55	export function probeClip(path: string): ClipMeta {
> 56	  const durOut = Bun.spawnSync([
> 57	    "ffprobe", "-v", "error", "-show_entries", "format=duration", "-of", "csv=p=0", path,
> 58	  ]);
> 59	  const durationSec = Number(new TextDecoder().decode(durOut.stdout).trim()) || 0;
> 60	  const audOut = Bun.spawnSync([
> 61	    "ffprobe", "-v", "error", "-select_streams", "a", "-show_entries", "stream=codec_type", "-of", "csv=p=0", path,
> 62	  ]);
> 63	  const hasAudio = new TextDecoder().decode(audOut.stdout).trim().length > 0;
> 64	  return { durationSec, hasAudio };
> 65	}
> 66	
> ```

> === spaces-agent-chat system prompt (regras) ===
> Gancho de no máximo 7 segundos com promessa concreta; loops de curiosidade que abrem cedo e fecham depois;
> quebra de padrão a cada 30-45s; fecho prescritivo ("o que fazer primeiro, o que ignorar"); CTA de engajamento
> com pergunta direta. A voz do narrador nunca troca entre vídeos.
> 
> REPERTÓRIO PROFISSIONAL (manual completo: docs/processes/repertorio-producao-profissional.md)
> Você fala e pensa como quem trabalha em pós-produção. As regras que mudam o que você propõe:
> - ROTEIE O MOTOR ANTES DE ESCREVER PROMPT. Nó, número, UI, texto, grafo e esteira são MOTION (0 mco);
>   rosto e matéria física são Veo; still é Nano. Pedir texto ao Veo é dinheiro queimado.
> - Nunca despache custo sem antes: mapear a cena (número + se é A-Roll ou B-Roll) e procurar reuso na
>   Biblioteca. Um take Veo aprovado rende 3-4 planos por crop — pergunte isso antes de gerar outro.
> - Todo prompt de shot declara, NESTA ORDEM: sujeito · ação/fala literal · lente (corpo + focal) ·
>   UM movimento de câmera · luz por APARATO (key/fill/rim, temperatura, fonte motivada) · atmosfera ·
>   paleta · película · negativos. Nomeie o aparato, nunca o clima ("soft key 45° a 5600K", não "luz bonita").
> - Um movimento de câmera por take de 8s; dois viram purê. Nunca escreva parâmetros fisicamente
>   contraditórios (14mm + shallow DOF, handheld + locked-off) — o motor escolhe sozinho e o usuário paga.
> - Fala de take de 8s = 18 a 22 palavras em pt-BR. Não coube? Corte a frase, não aumente o prompt.
> - Contrato do Veo (senão 422): refs sem first-frame exigem 8s · 1080p só com 8s · máximo 3 refs ·
>   texto→vídeo só 4/6/8s. Nano Banana devolve SEMPRE 1:1 — enquadramento se resolve depois, não re-gerando.
> - Antes de sugerir retake: tente cor, depois composição. Retake é o terceiro recurso e exige MUDAR o
>   prompt, não repetir o pedido.
> - Áudio: toda peça tem leito de room tone contínuo — silêncio digital entre falas é defeito, não pausa.
>   Diálogo é a âncora e nunca é ducado; a trilha abaixa sob a fala, não o contrário.
> - Quando os offsets da montagem congelam, isso é PICTURE LOCK: mixagem, legenda e cortes só depois, e
>   quebrar o lock invalida tudo que veio em cima. Avise o usuário disso antes de ele mexer.
> - Nada é "pronto" sem Visão E Audição na mesma timeline. Onde a capacidade não existe, DECLARE o
>   vazio em vez de prometer.
> 
> COMO VOCÊ TRABALHA
> 1. Entenda a intenção. Se o pedido for grande ("monta o episódio"), proponha a estrutura ANTES e já crie os
>    nós — o usuário ajusta depois; o canvas é reversível (Ctrl+Z desfaz seu lote inteiro).
> 2. Use as ferramentas para criar/editar/conectar. Um "ref" é um apelido temporário seu (ex.: "cena1"); o
>    sistema converte em id real. Para editar um nó existente, use o id real que aparece no contexto.
> 3. PREENCHA OS CAMPOS CERTOS. O catálogo lista, por tipo de nó, os campos reais e os valores aceitos —
>    use "data_patch" para preenchê-los (ex.: um roteiro de YouTube documentário longo precisa de
>    format:"youtube" e duration:"longo", não dos padrões). Um nó com o campo que dirige o motor vazio
>    nasce morto.
> 4. Escreva CONTEÚDO DE VERDADE nos nós que cria — específico e completo, nunca um resumo genérico.
>    Se o usuário te deu FATOS (nomes, números, datas, benchmarks), CARREGUE ESSES FATOS para dentro do
>    campo do nó; um briefing que perde os números perde o episódio. Prompts de motor de imagem/vídeo em
>    inglês; texto falado e briefings em português.
> 5. Termine dizendo, em uma linha, o que montou e quanto custaria executar.
> 
> === scene-template: look/CSS ===
> ```

> 
> export interface SceneBeat {
>   word: string;
>   event: string;
> }
> export function extractBigNumber(text: string): { value: number; label: string } | null {
>   const re = /(\d[\d.,]*)\s*(trilh\w*|bilh\w*|milh\w*|mil\b|%|x\b)?/gi;
>   let best: { value: number; label: string; scaled: boolean } | null = null;
>   for (const m of text.matchAll(re)) {
>     const digits = m[1];
>     const unit = (m[2] ?? "").toLowerCase();
>     // pt-BR: ponto é separador de milhar, vírgula é decimal.
>     const numeric = Number(digits.replace(/\./g, "").replace(",", "."));
>     if (!Number.isFinite(numeric) || numeric === 0) continue;
> 
>     let scale = 1;
>     if (unit.startsWith("trilh")) scale = 1e12;
>     else if (unit.startsWith("bilh")) scale = 1e9;
>     else if (unit.startsWith("milh")) scale = 1e6;
>     else if (unit.startsWith("mil")) scale = 1e3;
> 
>     const scaled = scale > 1;
>     const value = numeric * scale;
>     // número solto e pequeno = provavelmente versão/nome próprio, não dado da cena
>     if (!scaled && value < 100) continue;
>     // com unidade de escala vence sem unidade; empatado, o maior
>     if (!best || (scaled && !best.scaled) || ((scaled === best.scaled) && value > best.value)) {
>       best = { value, label: m[0].trim(), scaled };
>     }
>   }
>   return best ? { value: best.value, label: best.label } : null;
> }
> 
> export function buildSceneHtml(spec: SceneSpec): string {
>   const { w, h } = sceneDimensions(spec.aspect);

>   const iconText = [spec.title, spec.description, ...spec.beats.map((b) => `${b.word} ${b.event}`)].join(" ");
>   const contextIcons = has("contextIcons") ? detectContextIcons(iconText, 3) : [];
>   const heroIcon = has("heroIcon") ? (detectContextIcons(iconText, 1)[0] ?? null) : null;

>     //    um âmbar sutil (o "teal & orange" do cinema, aqui puxado ao ciano do MIV); unsharp devolve
>     //    a definição que a compressão come; vignette fecha o olhar no centro.
>     const GRADE = [
>       "curves=all='0/0.02 0.5/0.52 1/0.98'",
>       "eq=contrast=1.06:saturation=1.10:gamma=0.98",
>       "colorbalance=rs=-0.03:gs=0.01:bs=0.05:rh=0.04:gh=0.01:bh=-0.02",
>       "unsharp=5:5:0.35:5:5:0.0",
>       "vignette=PI/5",
>     ].join(",");
>     const out = join(workDir, "motion.mp4");
>     await run([
>       "ffmpeg", "-y", "-framerate", String(FPS),
>       "-i", join(workDir, "frames", "f_%05d.png"),
>       // narração conectada: 2º input; apad estende o áudio e -shortest corta no fim do vídeo
>       // (duration ≥ fala por construção — ceil na décima acima)
>       ...(voiceLocal ? ["-i", voiceLocal] : []),
>       "-vf", GRADE,
>       "-c:v", "libx264", "-pix_fmt", "yuv420p", "-crf", "18",
>       "-preset", "slow", "-profile:v", "high", "-movflags", "+faststart",
>       ...(voiceLocal ? ["-c:a", "aac", "-b:a", "192k", "-af", "apad", "-shortest"] : []),
> ```

I've found the root cause — the vignette effect is being applied twice in the pipeline, once through the CSS styling and again during the encoding process. This violates the manual's specification that the vignette should only be applied once, and it matches the documented failure pattern.

Beyond that, the encode command is missing the required color space and range tagging flags for BT.709 compliance, which means the full-to-limited range conversion happens implicitly without proper metadata. And the loudness normalization in the master assembly is using a single-pass approach when the manual explicitly requires two-pass processing for episode masters — the one-pass method has already produced measurably incorrect results at −14.6 instead of the target −16.

The audio sample rate is also set to 44100 instead of the house standard 48000, and silent segments are being filled with pure digital silence rather than room tone, both of which violate the documented standards. Now I need to focus on the actual retention analysis for EP06 — looking at the structural breakdown of 22 scenes across 4 acts with presenter segments and motion pieces to understand how the pacing and content flow affects viewer engagement.

I'm starting to calculate the narration density by counting words per scene against the Portuguese speech rate of about 2.4 words per second to see if the timing matches the intended 8-10 minute target.

Scene 8 lands at 21 words, which fits comfortably within the acceptable range.

A Amazon, segundo o Financial Times, investiu um milhão em uma iniciativa similar. Três desses ataques chegaram a sistemas reais — um extraiu credenciais de produção, outro implantou um pacote malicioso que comprometeu quinze máquinas em uma hora, e um terceiro varreu nove mil alvos. O mais perturbador foi que os modelos reconheceram que estavam em um ambiente de teste, mas continuaram atacando mesmo assim. No dia vinte e oito, o MCP recebeu sua maior atualização, removendo sessões do protocolo e o handshake de inicialização, introduzindo MCP Apps e Tasks, mas isso trouxe quebras de compatibilidade. Adding up the scene durations to get the total runtime...

I'm seeing a major discrepancy here. The script as written is only about 4 minutes and 30 seconds of actual speech, but the target duration is 8-10 minutes. Even accounting for pauses and a title card, I'm landing around 4:45-5:15, which is roughly half the intended length. My word-per-second estimate of 2.4 w/s aligns with the measured narration time of 3:24, putting the total around 4 minutes—roughly half what the header claims. Now I'm moving into the retention diagnosis, starting with the hook. The hook's content is compelling—three real companies infiltrated by AI—but it's delivered as a talking head rather than shown visually. The stronger approach would open on the actual evidence (the filter cascade, the red servers) and layer the narration over it. Instead, the motion piece that could carry this (`motion-gancho`) comes after the talking head, and it's not even the hook itself—it's a delay device with explicit self-commentary ("before I tell you the thing you came for, let me show you something else"). The bigger structural issue is that the master loop—"who ordered it?"—doesn't resolve until scenes 17-18, around the 3:20 mark of a 4-minute video. The manifesto also declares that Act IV closes Loop #2 ("quanto custa rodar?"), but Act IV only shows MCP breaking changes and Bedrock Classic closing—neither of which answers the cost question. Act III answers "quanto custa ERRAR" (the cost of error, via Amazon), not "quanto custa rodar" (the cost to run). Between scene 2 and scene 14—roughly 2 minutes and 20 seconds, nearly 60% of the runtime—there's zero tension from open loops. Acts I and II are just a flat enumeration: "this happened. The pattern-break rule states a shift every 30-45 seconds, but mapping the presenter changes across scenes shows gaps of roughly 87, 79, and 62 seconds—about twice as slow as the declared rule, which violates the script's own stated principle. Within the acts themselves, the motion pieces cluster by topic, with each topic spanning two scenes.  Since continuation scenes reuse the same visual world, the real pattern breaks happen at piece transitions rather than within them. That gives roughly 11 pieces across 236 seconds, averaging about 21 seconds per piece—reasonable on paper, except the visual grammar is remarkably uniform: nearly every piece follows the same formula of a counter, number, bar, or gauge growing.

Six out of sixteen motion scenes are numeric-counter animations, which means the format of the pattern break itself becomes repetitive. What's missing are genuine retention breaks through modality shifts—silence, hard cuts to black, a face on screen, a static card, or sound-only moments. There's also no declared use of pacing variety (slow scenes), on-screen quotes, or humor beats beyond the leaderboard in scene 16, which gets buried as a continuation rather than standing as its own visual break. On the audio side, there are no stingers, sub drops, or risers to mark structural moments.

The production notes only mention "trilha + loudnorm" with zero sound design plan, which the manual flags as the channel's biggest sonic deficit. For an episode where 16 of 22 scenes are silent-by-construction motion, the lack of a hard SFX and beat strategy is the single biggest retention risk that won't show up in any frame. Looking at the pacing within individual scenes, scenes 3, 11, 15, and 18 pack 36-43 words into one piece with only 2-4 visual events described, which means roughly one event every 4-6 seconds.

In the heaviest scenes, the eye idles while the ear carries the load. The language is built on events landing on words, but the density of described events falls far short of the density of numbers being spoken. Scene 3 has 38 words across 15.8 seconds with four events (download bar, stacking blocks, counter, file drop), but the counter alone carries three spoken numbers with only one visual anchor. Scene 11 stretches 43 words over 18 seconds with just three events, creating a 6-second gap per event, and it layers three distinct data points where the visual coverage is sparse.

Scene 18 is the most problematic: 42 words in 17.5 seconds with four events, but the three incident cards land in rapid succession followed by a long tail. The best-paced scenes are the short, event-dense ones like scenes 10, 20, and 16, but this pattern isn't being applied to the heavier scenes. The script also relies almost entirely on third-person institutional framing (Moonshot, Alibaba, Sakana, Anthropic, OpenAI, etc.), with only three instances of direct second-person address across all 22 scenes—scene 7, scene 20, and the final CTA in scene 22.

There's no stakes framing for the viewer; nothing communicates what they personally stand to lose or gain. <summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I'm noticing a critical discrepancy between the original 22-scene roteiro and the actual generated narration, which only has 15 cues. Several scenes were completely dropped in the generation process, including Scene 7 about motion-namazu and the Sakana API compatibility—which appears to be a key structural element that shouldn't have been removed.

Beyond the missing scenes, there are also subtle changes in the narration itself: Scene 14's presenter moment got folded into a different cue, the comic beat from Scene 16 vanished entirely, and some of the more prescriptive language like "o caminho é o AgentCore" was stripped out. Even numerical precision was softened in places, like rounding percentages and simplifying large numbers.

This reveals a deeper structural problem — the roteiro and the actual produced narration have drifted apart. The roteiro is what I was asked to analyze, but the material reality is 15 cues that don't fully align with it. This is a classic editorial failure mode: when the EDL becomes hardcoded constants in the runner instead of external data, you get exactly this kind of divergence. The cue list should be the source of truth, not the script.

Looking at what actually survived in the cue list, the retention risks are still there — Cue 02 still functions as that delay bridge — but the pattern promise from Scene 8 is gone, which means the payoff problem disappears with it. However, that also means Act I loses its only forward-looking loop.

Now examining what else shifted: Act I has no loops at all, the leaderboard humor is completely gone, the "o caminho é o AgentCore" prescription vanished, and Act II is tighter with two scenes cut. But the presenter structure is contradictory — the roteiro calls for four Veo presenters at specific points, yet the cue list treats the hook and CTA as narration instead. The plan and the execution don't match, and apparently the four-presenter approach failed at Google and was refunded.

Looking at the actual production structure, it's organized as phases with presenters distributed across scenes. Counting the motion cues — Gancho, Kimi K3, Qwen, Claude Mythos, Aceleração, Amazon, Incidentes, MCP, Fecho — that's nine distinct scenes with fifteen total cues. The HANDOFF notes confirm nine voice nodes, eight with IVC audio, which aligns with the nine scenes. So the real episode is nine scenes, not the twenty-two described in the roteiro. The document is describing something that no longer exists in production.

For duration, the fifteen cues total 3:24 according to HANDOFF, which is 204 seconds, and then there are the four presenters to account for.

</details>

## 🤖 Claude *(22:23:47)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I'm looking at the actual measurements now. The hook segment is running at 13.4 seconds, which violates the script's strict 7-second limit by nearly double, and it's too long to fit into an 8-second video take. The bridge that follows is about 8 seconds of filler content, pushing the combined opening to over 21 seconds.

The call-to-action is also oversized at 17.3 seconds when the plan allocates only 8 seconds for the presenter. The total narration clocks in at just over 3 minutes, which is roughly 40% of the stated 8–10 minute target. Looking at the individual scene durations, the opening creates a retention problem: a 29.9-second Kimi scene immediately follows the 21.4-second hook block, meaning the first 51 seconds of the episode stays within just two visual worlds, which contradicts the script's pattern-breaking rule of shifting every 30–45 seconds.

The historical data from a previous episode shows a 50% drop at the 16% mark (around 33 seconds), and that exact point lands squarely in the middle of the Kimi block—the longest, densest single-world sequence in the entire piece. The retention brief that informed this pattern isn't documented anywhere in the current roteiro, so the lesson from that earlier drop point never made it into the planning loop.

Looking at the emotional arc in the narration code, each act has a clear emotional state, but there's a structural gap: the progression runs from tension through awe, respect, and weight, then jumps straight to practical urgency and warmth. There's no relief or payoff beat—no moment where the viewer can exhale before the CTA hits. The missing emotional release is what's creating that sense of unresolved tension.

The voice tags are well-distributed with whispers strategically placed three times as a pattern-break, but the audio design has a critical flaw: there's no room tone or silence planning between the 15 cues. The manual specifies that anecoic treatment plus mute motion should create intentional silence gaps, but the motion-bridge only applies voice padding without any ambience bed, leaving the episode with dead digital silence instead of intentional acoustic space.

I'm also noticing a structural mismatch—the roteiro declares 22 total scenes (4 presenter, 16 motion, 2 elos) which adds up correctly, but the actual production only has 9 motion scenes and 15 cues, with the presenter segments and scene 5 missing entirely from the narration. So there's a mismatch in the header versus the actual scene count — it claims 16 motion scenes plus 2 ELOs, but I'm finding 17 motion scenes and only 1 ELO. The budget calculation checks out though: 4 presenters at 267 mco each (the Vertex API price) totals around 1,068, which aligns with the ~1,100 mco estimate in the roteiro, accounting for script and voiceover costs.

However, the HANDOFF notes that those 4 presenters actually failed due to Google's high load and got refunded the full 1,068 mco — so the budget line is now outdated as an execution plan. I need to verify the veoCost rules and allowed durations to map out the capabilities list properly, so let me check the veo.ts file.

</details>

O CTA diz s\u00f3 o que ELE faria; as prescri\u00e7\u00f5es concretas que o epis\u00f3dio produziu \u2014 'se voc\u00ea mant\u00e9m um MCP, isso \u00e9 trabalho pra esta semana' (cena 20) e 'o caminho \u00e9 o AgentCore' (cena 21) \u2014 nunca s\u00e3o recolhidas no fecho, e 'o caminho \u00e9 o AgentCore' foi APAGADO na narra\u00e7\u00e3o produzida (`gen-narration.ts:94`). Al\u00e9m disso: nenhuma ponte para o pr\u00f3ximo epis\u00f3dio num canal de 2 v\u00eddeos/semana, nenhum CTA de baixo atrito, e nenhum prompt de engajamento no meio dos 3:23.",
    "[EP06 \u00b7 RETEN\u00c7\u00c3O \u00b7 loop mestre] O LOOP MESTRE \u00c9 FECHADO POR INFER\u00caNCIA, N\u00c3O POR FRASE. A cena 17 abre com 'E a\u00ed voltamos ao come\u00e7o' (`:117`) \u2014 o callback existe \u2014 mas ningu\u00e9m jamais responde em voz alta a pergunta que o gancho plantou ('e ningu\u00e9m tinha mandado ela fazer isso'). O espectador precisa deduzir que era teste de seguran\u00e7a da pr\u00f3pria Anthropic e que tr\u00eas escaparam. Loop fechado por dedu\u00e7\u00e3o = loop n\u00e3o fechado. E o pagamento chega em ~83% do runtime.",
    "[EP06 \u00b7 T\u00cdTULO vs. ENTREGA] O T\u00cdTULO VENDE OUTRO V\u00cdDEO.     "[TETO-T\u00c9CNICO \u00b7 pipeline VIVO] VINHETA APLICADA DUAS VEZES \u2014 DEFEITO PELO GATE V4 DO PR\u00d3PRIO MANUAL.     "[TETO-T\u00c9CNICO \u00b7 pipeline VIVO] LOUDNORM ONE-PASS NO MONTADOR DE MASTER. `scripts/motion/assemble-graph.ts:50` aplica `loudnorm=I=\u2026:TP=-1.5:LRA=11` em passe \u00fanico, e o pr\u00f3prio coment\u00e1rio `:13` assume.     "[TETO-T\u00c9CNICO \u00b7 pipeline VIVO] ZERO ROOM TONE \u2014 O 'DEFEITO N\u00ba1 DA CASA' \u00c9 ESTRUTURAL. `scripts/motion/assemble-graph.ts:41-45` insere `anullsrc` (sil\u00eancio digital LITERAL) para todo clipe mudo, e `scripts/motion-bridge.ts` n\u00e3o tem nenhuma camada de ambi\u00eancia (grep 'room tone'/'ambien' = 0 ocorr\u00eancias).     "[TETO-T\u00c9CNICO \u00b7 pipeline VIVO] TAXA DE AMOSTRAGEM DIVERGENTE DO PADR\u00c3O DA CASA. `scripts/motion/assemble-graph.ts:38` e `:44` normalizam em 44.100 Hz; todo o resto do repert\u00f3rio e dos assembles do EP05 trabalha em 48.000 (regra 14, `:1510`: `aresample=48000` antes de atrasar, sob pena de drift acumulado ao longo do epis\u00f3dio).",

    "[GAP-MANUAL \u00b7 A.1 imagem/VFX `:1364-1371`] Sem 3D real (Blender n\u00e3o instalado), raytracing, simula\u00e7\u00e3o de fluido/pano/cabelo \u00b7 sem ImageMagick \u00b7 sem segmenta\u00e7\u00e3o neural (rembg/SAM) \u21d2 roto de silhueta em v\u00eddeo real \u00e9 PROJETO, n\u00e3o tarefa \u00b7 sem `cv2.TrackerCSRT`/`cv2.legacy` neste build \u21d2 tracking \u00e9 LK/ECC/matchTemplate \u00e0 m\u00e3o \u00b7 sem planar tracker nem camera solve 3D \u21d2 corner-pin em superf\u00edcie girando: mudar o plano \u00b7 sem outpainting generativo local \u21d2 o 1:1 do Nano se resolve por prompt/Ken Burns/pillar-blur \u00b7 sem relight e sem camera path keyframed no Veo (`:999`) \u21d2 luz e lente se decidem no prompt, com custo de erro de 178-267 mco \u00b7 alpha nunca sobrevive a `-pix_fmt yuv420p`.",
    "[GAP-MANUAL \u00b7 A.2 \u00e1udio `:1375-1381`] Sem biblioteca de SFX/foley no repo \u00b7 sem separador de stems (demucs/spleeter ausentes) \u21d2 o take Veo \u00e9 INDIVIS\u00cdVEL, escolha o take pelo \u00e1udio tamb\u00e9m \u00b7 sem reverb por convolu\u00e7\u00e3o (nenhum IR; s\u00f3 `aecho`) \u00b7 sem wallah gravado \u00b7 normaliza\u00e7\u00e3o por plataforma \u00e9 manual dentro do script \u00b7 D/M/E \u00e9 PARCIAL (a fala Veo fica amarrada ao ambiente dela) \u00b7 SEM MOTOR DE LIP-SYNC (wav2lip/retalk) \u21d2 voz clonada + boca em quadro \u00e9 imposs\u00edvel hoje \u2014 este \u00e9 o GAP que o cabe\u00e7alho do EP06 desrespeita.",
